B. Page Brief

{
  "year": 1978,
  "page_type": "department letter",
  "page_slug": "editor_letter",
  "title": "Editor Letter",
  "subtitle": "Letter From the Editor",
  "writer": {
    "name": "BJ Lovestreet, Editor-in-Chief",
    "byline": "By BJ Lovestreet, Editor-in-Chief"
  },
  "source_files": [
    "PROJECT_STATE.md",
    "issues/context/1978_context_enriched.json",
    "issues/1978/layout/1978_production_targets.json",
    "issues/1978/layout/1978_issue_map.json",
    "issues/1978/editor_letter.md"
  ],
  "page_purpose": "Present Editor Letter as a complete RetroVerse 1978 department letter page, with page-aware artwork under real typography and no text baked into the illustration layer for `editor_letter`.",
  "story_summary": "Editor Letter Letter From the Editor There is a certain kind of opening sound that can define a year before you even finish the first line of the chorus. In this issue's dataset, \"Stayin' Alive\" by Bee Gees stands at the top with a score of 503, peak position #1, and 23 chart weeks. The numbers are clear, but the feeling matters just as much: 1978 moved with confidence. A dramatic urban parkway under rain with disco bravado, wind, and storm-lit movement pushing the scene toward spectacle.

Scene description: A dramatic urban parkway under rain with disco bravado, wind, and storm-lit movement pushing the scene toward spectacle.
Camera style: dynamic diagonal action frame
Lighting style: stormy backlight with sodium-vapor highlights

Editorial enrichment:
Cultural context: As the No. 7 song of 1978 with 17 chart weeks and a No. 1 peak, the location title gives it a larger frame than a simple love song, making it feel cinematic even inside a singles chart.
Sound description: The name and chart profile suggest sweeping scale, dramatic build, and a sense of motion across a whole public scene.
Notable fact: It reached No. 1 and stayed on the chart for 17 weeks, giving it both headline status and shelf life.
Scene use: Use it in wide park views, stormy skies, city monuments, or weather moving across a public space.

Writing guidance:
Write a vivid, specific magazine-style article using concrete details. Avoid vague phrases like 'became popular' or 'part of the culture.' Reference real-world context, scenes, or usage when possible.

Create a lively illustrated scene reflecting the topic.
1970s editorial illustration style.
MAD Magazine caricature influence.
comic line art + watercolor textures.
late 1970s neon palette.
handwritten signature "Judge" as a small artist mark only.

Important:
leave soft areas suitable for text placement.
Do not include readable typography.
